### Rosicrucians
<note>original: Rosenkreutzer. The Rosicrucians were a secret philosophical and mystical society said to have been founded in late medieval Germany, which gained significant public attention in the early 17th century through the publication of several manifestos.</note>

### HERMETIC PHILOSOPHY
<note>original: Philosophia Hermetica. This refers to a philosophical and esoteric tradition based on writings attributed to Hermes Trismegistus. It explores the hidden connections between the spirit and the material world, and was a foundational pillar of Rosicrucian thought and alchemy.</note>

[Ritter]
<note>original: Ritter. This is the German word for "Knight." It may refer to the owner of the book, a specific contributor, or a grade/rank within a chivalric or secret order.</note>
</insert>

<note>This page features a hand-colored emblem depicting a "Pelican in its Piety." In this traditional symbolic image, a pelican is shown wounding its own breast to feed its young with its blood. In an alchemical or Rosicrucian context, this represents the "Great Work" (the Magnum Opus) and the spirit of self-sacrifice and purification. Below the pelican is a heraldic shield with five roses, which is a direct reference to the "Rose Cross" or Rosicrucian symbolism.</note>

--- Page 7 ---
<meta>This page serves as the formal title page for the 1654 herbal and astronomical calendar. It follows the blank flyleaf described previously. The layout is typical of the mid-17th century, using varied font sizes and decorative spacing to emphasize the author and the utility of the work.</meta>

# **Newer Root and Herb Calendar/**
## the likes of which have never been seen before:

In which not only (as is otherwise the case in the
other common ones) the holy feast and holidays / the course / the path /
together with the bad and good aspects <note>In astrology, an "aspect" is the angle planets make to each other, believed to influence earthly events and the potency of medicinal plants.</note> of the planets / as well as the weather
and natural selections <note>The "election" refers to choosing the most auspicious time to perform an action, such as planting or harvesting.</note> of many noble and magnificent growths of roots / herbs
and flowers / the natural right time to dig / to pluck and to gather / but also in part
the nature / signs and properties of animals and humans / together with beautiful histories
are placed and shown before your eyes / for the year after
the birth of our Savior **Jesus Christ**

---

# **1654**
<note>original: M. DC. LIV.</note>

**Brought together with diligence for the benefit and special instruction of everyone / and prepared for print**

->By<-

**Marx Friederich Rosenkreutzer / a spe-**
**cial lover of natural magic** <note>original: Magiæ</note> **and the**
**astronomical arts.**

> Solomon in the Song of Songs, chapter 7.
> Come, my friend / let us go
> out into the field / that we may
> see / if the vine blossoms and
> has gained buds / whether the
> pomegranates have sprouted.
> The lilies give forth their scent /
> and before our door are all
> kinds of noble fruits.

**With the Privilege of His Roman Imperial Majesty.** <note>This "Privilege" functioned as an early form of copyright and royal endorsement.</note>
->**Nuremberg/**<-
**Printed and published by Wolfgang**
**Endter / the elder.**

<summary>This is the title page of a 1654 botanical and astronomical calendar by Marx Friederich Rosenkreutzer, published in Nuremberg. It promises instructions on the medicinal harvesting of plants based on planetary movements and biblical wisdom.</summary>

--- Page 9 ---
<meta>This page transitions from the blank verso of the previous year (1659) to a text-heavy introductory page for the year 1654. Although the previous page’s bleed-through suggested a later date, this specific leaf belongs to a 1654 almanac. It provides a chronological breakdown of time since major biblical and historical events, followed by a comparative table between the Old (Julian) and New (Gregorian) calendars.</meta>

<note>Pen scribbles and ink stains appear on the left side of the calendar comparison section.</note>

# This present year, which
is counted from the <term>Incarnation</term><note>The Christian doctrine of God becoming human in the person of Jesus Christ.</note> and grace-filled birth of our LORD and Savior JESUS Christ, the year 1654.
It is, from the creation of Heaven and Earth—according to the calculation of Mr. Calvisius <note>Sethus Calvisius (1556–1615) was a famous German astronomer and chronologer.</note> and other most highly proven scholars—5603. From the powerful and universal Great Flood, 3947. From the very first Passover and Pentecost festivals after the departure of the children of Israel out of Egypt, 3151. From the suffering, death, resurrection, and ascension of JESUS Christ, 1621. From the beginning of the old <term>Julian Calendar</term><note>The calendar system established by Julius Caesar in 46 BC.</note>, 1698. From the new, corrected <term>Gregorian Calendar</term><note>The calendar introduced by Pope Gregory XIII in 1582 to correct the drift of the Julian year; it was adopted at different times by different regions, leading to the "Old" and "New" styles seen here.</note>, 72 years.

### In this year, however, according to the:

| Old Calendar | | New Calendar |
|:---|:---|:---|
| 2 | <term>Golden Number</term><note>original: "Aureus numerus." A number used in a 19-year cycle to determine the date of the new moon.</note> | 2 |
| 11 | <term>Solar Cycle</term><note>original: "Ciclus Solaris." A 28-year cycle used to determine the day of the week for any given date.</note> | 11 |
| **F.** | <term>Dominical Letter</term><note>original: "Litera Dominicalis." A letter from A to G assigned to a year to indicate which day Sunday falls on.</note> | **D.** |
| 7 | <term>Roman Indiction</term><note>original: "Indictio Romana." A 15-year cycle originally used for tax assessment in the Roman Empire.</note> | 7 |
| 22 | <term>Epacts</term><note>original: "Epactæ." The age of the moon in days on January 1st, used to calculate the date of Easter.</note> | 12 |
| 6 weeks, 0 days. | Small Interval: Between Christmas and <term>Shrove Tuesday</term><note>original: "Herrn Fastnacht." The period of carnival before the beginning of Lent.</note> | 7 weeks, 3 days. |

<summary>This page serves as a chronological preface for the year 1654, calculating the time elapsed since the Creation, the Flood, and the birth of Christ. It also provides a technical comparison of calendar variables between the Julian and Gregorian systems.</summary>
<keywords>1654, chronology, Sethus Calvisius, Julian calendar, Gregorian calendar, Golden Number, Epacts, Christian era</keywords>

--- Page 11 ---
<meta>This page provides the specific astrological positions of the planets for the start of 1654 and the month of January. It transitions from the calendar grid of the previous page to technical data and folk wisdom regarding medical botany and weather lore.</meta>

<header>Position of the planets at the beginning of this year and month. January has 31 days.</header>

# Position of the planets at the beginning of this year and month.

# January has 31 days.

---

The Sun is on the 1st of this month at 20 degrees 45 minutes of Capricorn in the face <note>original: "Angesicht." In astrology, a 'face' or decan is a 10-degree subdivision of a zodiac sign that helps determine a planet's strength.</note>.

Saturn is at 25 degrees 31 minutes of Leo and is still **retrograde** <note>original: "Krebsgängig," literally "walking like a crab."</note>; it rises 3 hours after sunset.

Jupiter is at 20 degrees 31 minutes of Aquarius in his bounds; he can still be seen for a short time after sunset.

Mars is at 15 degrees 4 minutes of Pisces in his double **triplicity** <note>original: "Triplicität." An astrological system that groups zodiac signs into four elements (fire, earth, air, water).</note>; on the 21st of this month, he enters Aries, his daily house and face; he can be seen quite beautifully high up in the 9th house <note>The 9th house of a birth chart or astrological map traditionally governs travel, philosophy, and religion.</note> after sunset.

Venus is at 4 degrees 56 minutes of Pisces, her exaltation and bounds; on the 24th, she also enters Aries into her harmful **exile** <note>original: "Exilium." Also known as "detriment," this is a position where a planet is considered weak because it is in the sign opposite the one it rules.</note>; she shines for over 3 hours after sunset.

Mercury is at 26 degrees 27 minutes of Capricorn and will return to a direct course on the 19th of this month; he can be seen in the middle of this month.

-> * <-

If one wishes to have <term>herbs</term>, flowers, seeds, roots, and other things for <term>medicine</term> <note>original: "Arzney"</note>, they must be <term>collected</term> <note>original: "colligirt"</note> and gathered with great caution at the right time, when each is in its best power, virtue, and effect; otherwise, they do not perform what is praised and written of them. For some herbs are most powerful during <term>Rogation Week</term> <note>original: "Creutzwochen." The week leading up to the feast of the Ascension.</note>, some during the <term>dog days</term> <note>original: "Hundstagen." The hottest days of summer, typically in July and August.</note>, and some between the two <term>Feasts of Our Lady</term> <note>original: "Frauen Tagen." Likely referring to the period between the Assumption (August 15) and the Nativity of Mary (September 8), traditionally a peak time for gathering medicinal plants.</note>. <term>Theophrastus</term> <note>Referring to Theophrastus von Hohenheim, better known as the physician and occultist Paracelsus.</note> says that if one wants to collect a herb, it should be done from the 20th day of any month until the 30th. Some maintain that herbs gathered in the first seven days of the month are useful for children alone. In the second <term>septenary</term> <note>original: "Septenario." A period or cycle of seven days.</note>, for young men or maidens. In the third septenary, for men and women. In the fourth, however, for the elderly. But they should all be collected early before sunrise and when the days are not bright and clear; the best are those that grow on mountains where the wind can blow through them.

In this month, one should pay close attention to the day of the <term>Conversion of Paul</term> <note>January 25th.</note>. If the weather on that day is beautiful, bright, and clear, then a good year is to be hoped for. But if it is foggy, a time of death <note>Often referring to a plague or high mortality rate.</note> shall follow. If it rains or snows, it signifies a time of high prices <note>original: "theuer Zeit." Usually implying crop failure leading to expensive food.</note>, and if it is windy, war and rebellion shall follow.

<summary>This page details the astrological positions of the planets for January 1654 and provides traditional instructions for gathering medicinal herbs and predicting the year's events based on the weather on the Feast of the Conversion of Saint Paul.</summary>
<keywords>astrology, planetary positions, medicinal herbs, botany, Paracelsus, folklore, weather lore, January, 1654, St. Paul's Day</keywords>

<meta>This page continues the specific astrological and prognostic data for January 1654. While the previous page provided a day-by-day calendar grid, this page offers a narrative overview of planetary positions and traditional rules for gathering medicinal herbs and predicting the year's events based on specific feast days.</meta>

->January has 31 days.<-

## Position of the Planets at the Beginning of this Year and Month.

The Sun on the 1st of this month is at 20 degrees, 45 minutes of Capricorn in its Face <note>original: "Angesicht." In astrology, a "face" or decan is a subdivision of a zodiac sign spanning 10 degrees.</note>.
Saturn is at 25 degrees, 31 minutes of Leo and is still moving <term>retrograde</term><note>original: "Krebsgängig," literally "walking like a crab." This describes the optical illusion where a planet appears to move backward in the sky.</note>; it rises 3 hours after sunset.
Jupiter is at 20 degrees, 31 minutes of Aquarius in its Bounds <note>original: "Gränze." The "bounds" or "terms" are specific degrees within a sign where a planet has particular influence.</note>; it can still be seen for a short time after sunset.
Mars is at 15 degrees, 4 minutes of Pisces in its double <term>Triplicity</term><note>A group of three zodiac signs belonging to the same element; Mars has special "dignity" or strength here.</note>; on the 21st of this month, it enters Aries, its daily house and face; it can be seen beautifully high in the 9th house <note>A division of the sky in an astrological chart.</note> after sunset.
Venus is at 4 degrees, 56 minutes of Pisces, its Exaltation <note>A position where a planet's influence is most harmoniously expressed.</note> and Bounds; on the 24th, it also enters Aries into its harmful <term>Exile</term><note>original: "Exilium." Also known as "detriment," this is the sign opposite the one a planet rules, where its influence is considered weakened or corrupted.</note>; it shines for over 3 hours after sunset.
Mercury is at 26 degrees, 27 minutes of Capricorn and will return to direct motion <note>original: "richtiges Lauffs." Moving forward again after a period of being retrograde.</note> on the 19th; it can be seen in the middle of this month.

---

If one wishes to have herbs, flowers, seeds, roots, and other things for medicine, they must be <term>collected</term><note>original: "colligirt," from the Latin *colligere*.</note> and gathered with great caution at the right time, when each is at its best power, virtue, and effect; otherwise, they will not do what is praised and written of them. For some herbs are most powerful during the <term>Rogation Weeks</term><note>original: "Creutzwochen." The weeks surrounding Ascension Day, traditionally a time for processions and blessing the fields.</note>, some during the <term>Dog Days</term><note>original: "Hundstage." The hottest days of summer, usually from July to August, associated with the rising of the star Sirius.</note>, and some between the two <term>Lady Days</term><note>original: "Frauen Tagen." Likely referring to the period between the Assumption (August 15) and the Nativity of Mary (September 8), a traditional window for gathering medicinal plants.</note>. <term>Theophrastus</term><note>Theophrastus Paracelsus (1493–1541), a Swiss physician and alchemist who revolutionized medicine by emphasizing observation and the use of minerals and herbs.</note> says that if one wishes to <term>collect</term> a herb, it should happen from the 20th day of any month until the 30th. Some maintain that herbs gathered in the first seven days of the month are only useful for children. In the second <term>seven-day period</term><note>original: "Septenario."</note> for young men or maidens. In the third <term>seven-day period</term> for men and women. But in the fourth, for the elderly. They should all, however, be <term>collected</term> early before sunrise and when there are fine, bright, clear days; and the best are those that stand upon mountains, where the wind can blow through them well.

In this month, one should pay close attention to the day of the <term>Conversion of Saint Paul</term><note>original: "Pauli Bekehrung." January 25th. This was a critical "lost day" used in folk meteorology to predict the coming year.</note>; if the weather on that day is fine, bright, and clear, then a good year is to be hoped for. But if it is foggy, a time of death <note>Referring to plague or general high mortality.</note> shall follow. If it rains or snows, it signifies expensive times <note>Meaning crop failure and high food prices.</note>; and if it is windy, then war and rebellion shall follow.

It concludes with folk weather prognostications for the year based on the conditions on the Feast of the Conversion of St. Paul.</summary>
<keywords>astrology, planetary positions, herbalism, Paracelsus, Rogation Weeks, Dog Days, Saint Paul's Day, weather lore, medical gathering, 17th-century science</keywords>

<meta>This page continues the astrological and botanical observations for the year 1654. While the previous page focused on the calendar grid of February, this page provides a detailed narrative of planetary movements for the month and explores the natural history of the boxwood tree, a common feature in early modern almanacs.</meta>

# Position of the Planets at the Beginning of this Month.
# February has 28 Days.

---

The Sun enters the 22nd degree and 13th minute of **Aquarius** on the 1st of this month, being in its **Exile**. <note>original: "Exilio." In astrology, a planet is in "exile" or detriment when it is in the zodiac sign opposite the one it rules. The Sun rules Leo, so it is "weak" in Aquarius.</note>

**Saturn**, at the 23rd degree and 9th minute of **Leo**, is still in **retrograde** <note>original: "Krebsgang," literally "crab-walk," referring to the apparent backward motion of a planet.</note> / will be positioned opposite the Sun on the 2nd of this month / will follow the Sun hereafter / and can be seen at sunset in the 12th House near the **Heart of the Lion**. <note>original: "Löwenhertz," the star Regulus, the brightest star in the constellation Leo.</note>

**Jupiter** now begins at the 27th degree and 58th minute of **Aquarius** to join its own nocturnal house of **Pisces**, where it will arrive on the 11th of this month; it reaches the Sun on the 8th and will henceforth precede it.

**Mars** is in the 8th degree and 7th minute of **Aries**, its own diurnal house, in which it will remain throughout this entire month; it can be recognized as exceedingly beautiful high in the 9th House at sunset, near **Venus**.

**Venus**, at the 8th degree and 1st minute of **Aries**, enters **Taurus**, her nocturnal house and diurnal **Triplicity** <note>An astrological grouping of three signs belonging to the same element.</note>, on the day of **St. Matthias** <note>February 24th.</note>; she is to be found, as mentioned, up near **Mars**.

**Mercury**, at the 26th degree and 7th minute of **Capricorn**, runs into **Aquarius** on the 4th of this month and into **Pisces** on the 23rd; it is 28 degrees from the Sun on the 3rd and can be clearly seen.

->⁂<-

In this month, one gathers **Boxwood**, which grows readily in cold places under the open sky; it rises to the size of a standard tree with **myrtle leaves**, which are small, round, and evergreen. Its blossom is green, and the seed is red and round, repugnant to all animals. The wood is pale yellow and hard, as well as thick and heavy, so that it does not float on the water but falls to the bottom; it is also not pierced by worms nor does it grow old; it is as hard as iron and useful for neither fire nor charcoal, nor does it become light even when dry. Carvers know best how to make use of it. Boxes are also made from it, from which it takes its name. <note>In German, "Büchse" (box/container) and "Buchsbaum" (Boxwood) share the same linguistic root.</note> **Boxwood** has a constricting and drying nature. Its use is minor in **medicine**. **Boxwood leaves**, dried and powdered and taken with **lavender water**, takes away **insanity** or **frenzy of the brain**. <note>original: "Hirnwüten."</note> To sleep or rest under a **boxwood tree** diminishes one's **reason**, for its scent resists human nature.

<summary>This page details the astrological positions of the Sun and planets for February 1654 and provides a botanical profile of the boxwood tree, including its physical properties, use in carving, and medicinal effects on the brain.</summary>
<keywords>astrology, planetary positions, February, Boxwood, medicinal herbs, insanity, woodcarving, Regulus, retrograde</keywords>

<meta>This page continues the astrological and botanical guide for March 1654. While the previous page provided a day-by-day calendar of aspects and weather, this page offers a summary of the planetary positions at the start of the month and transitions into seasonal herbal advice, specifically concerning the anemone.</meta>

<header>Position of the planets at the start of this month. March has XXXI days.</header>
->B<- <note>This "B" is a signature mark, used by early modern printers to keep the large sheets of paper in the correct order for binding.</note>

# Position of the planets at the start
### of this month.

# March has XXXI days.

---

The ☉ <note>Sun</note> is on the 1st of this month in the 20th degree and 21st minute of Pisces.
♄ <note>Saturn</note> in the 20th degree and 56th minute of ♌ <note>Leo</note> is still in its retrograde <note>Apparent backward motion across the sky</note> motion; it can be seen after sunset between the 12th and 11th houses <note>The "houses" are twelve divisions of the sky used in astrology to determine a planet's influence.</note>.
♃ <note>Jupiter</note> in the 4th degree and 16th minute of ♓ <note>Pisces</note> is still under the ☉ <note>Sun's</note> rays; it precedes the Sun and can already be seen by the end of this month.
♂ <note>Mars</note> in the 28th degree and 29th minute of ♈ <note>Aries</note> moves on the 4th of this month into ♉ <note>Taurus</note>, his <term>Exilium</term> <note>Exile (or detriment): a position in the zodiac opposite the sign a planet naturally rules, where its influence was historically thought to be weakened.</note>; when the ☉ <note>Sun</note> goes to rest, one can clearly see him high in the 8th house.
♀ <note>Venus</note> in the 4th degree and 23rd minute of ♉ <note>Taurus</note> begins to stall in her course; she joins with ♂ <note>Mars</note> on the 25th of this month, and on the 29th she becomes retrograde; however, at the end of this month, she can still be seen in the 7th house with her lover <note>The author personifies the conjunction of Venus and Mars as a meeting of lovers.</note>.
☿ <note>Mercury</note> in the 12th degree and 31st minute of ♓ <note>Pisces</note> reaches the ☉ <note>Sun</note> on the 9th, enters ♈ <note>Aries</note> on the 11th, and enters ♉ <note>Taurus</note> on Easter Monday; thus he touches three signs.

---

At this time, it is customary to collect the little anemone roses; there are now two kinds of these. The first grows in cultivated places; some bring forth red, some purple, some white, and some yellow flowers. The leaves are not unlike coriander, but are more delicate and jagged. The stem is thin and woolly, having its flower at the top like the <term>oil poppy</term> <note>Original: "Oelmagen," referring to the opium poppy (Papaver somniferum), known for its distinct seed head.</note>, out of which blue or black buds emerge, in which the seed is enclosed; and these are called anemone roses. They grow in sandy and hilly places, in the woods and thorn hedges.
Both kinds are of a sharp nature, warm and dry <note>These are Galenic "humors," or qualities used to categorize medicine in the 17th century.</note>, almost like the hare's-foot clover. For this reason, they are used more outside the body than internally. The root, when chewed, draws out slimy moisture; it cleanses foul, stinking ulcers and heals rotting flesh. Distilled water <note>Original: "gebrandtes Wasser," an herbal distillate or hydrosol.</note> made from it does the same. The green leaves, crushed with their juice, consume the coarse, misshapen nails on the hands.

<summary>This page describes the astrological positions of the planets for March 1654 and provides a botanical profile of the anemone, including its physical characteristics and medicinal uses for skin conditions and hygiene.</summary>
<keywords>astrology, planetary positions, zodiac, March, anemone, herbal medicine, Galenic humors, pharmacology, 17th-century science</keywords>

<meta>This page continues the astrological and medicinal guide for the year 1654. Following the calendar grid for April, this section provides the specific celestial coordinates for the planets at the month's start, followed by botanical advice for the same period.</meta>

## Position of the Planets at the Beginning of this Month.
## April has 30 Days.

---

The Sun is on the 1st of this month in the 20th degree, 58th minute of Aries, in its exaltation, daily triplicity, and face <note>These are technical astrological terms: 'exaltation' refers to a sign where a planet's influence is strengthened; 'triplicity' and 'face' (or decan) are further subdivisions of the zodiac that refine planetary power.</note>.
Saturn, in the 29th degree, 28th minute of Leo, moves forward correctly again <note>original: "wider richtig für sich," meaning it has ended its retrograde period</note> on the 15th of this month, and can be clearly seen after sunset at the end of the 11th House.
Jupiter, in the 11th degree, 13th minute of Pisces, precedes the Sun, but cannot be seen due to its southern latitude <note>original: "mittägiger Breite," referring to its position relative to the celestial equator</note>.
Mars, in the 20th degree, 28th minute of Taurus, can still be recognized for a little while during the twilight after sunset; on the 15th of this month, it moves into Gemini.
Venus is retrograde in the 15th degree, 29th minute of Taurus; likewise, at the beginning of this month, it can only be seen for a short time.
Mercury is in the 8th degree, 5th minute of Taurus, and turns retrograde on the 21st of this month.

-> ❈ <-

In this month, it is customary to collect **Pellitory-of-Spain** <term>Bertram: *Anacyclus pyrethrum*, a root known since antiquity for its pungent, heat-inducing properties</term>; when held in the mouth, it draws out bad humors <note>original: "Feuchtung," referring to the excess fluids or "moistures" believed to cause illness in Galenic medicine</note> and takes away the pain of the teeth. **Pellitory** boiled with **wine** and **olive oil** <term>Baumöl: literally "tree oil," the standard term for olive oil in old German pharmacopoeia</term>, and rubbed onto paralyzed limbs, helps well; it also serves those who have **gout** in their feet, when applied as a **plaster**.
A **salve** made with **Pellitory** and **chamomile oil**, used to anoint the **spine** <term>Ruckmeissel: an archaic term for the backbone or spinal column</term> and covered warmly, prevents the shivering of **fever** and the **falling sickness** <note>A historical term for epilepsy</note>, especially in young children. **Pellitory** crushed and steeped in **brandy** <term>gebrannten Wein: distilled wine or spirits</term>, and rubbed onto a paralyzed tongue, restores lost speech. In short, whatever **ailments** <term>Bresten: infirmities or physical defects</term> are "cold" in nature may be warmed again with this root.

---

<meta>This page continues the astrological and medicinal calendar for May 1654. The previous page provided the daily calendar grid for the beginning of May. This page details the specific positions of the planets as the month begins and offers seasonal health advice and herbal recipes tied to the astrological sign of Taurus.</meta>

<header>May has 31 Days.</header>
<warning>The right half of the page contains a calendar grid that is severely faded and appears as ghosting from the reverse side; it is largely illegible and has been omitted to preserve accuracy. Transcription focuses on the clearly printed text on the left.</warning>

# May has **XXXI** Days.

### Positions of the Planets at the Beginning of this Month.

The <term>Sun</term> on the 1st of this month is at 20 degrees, 9 minutes of <term>Taurus</term> (♉︎).
<term>Saturn</term> is located at 29 degrees, 14 minutes of <term>Cancer</term> (♋︎) in the evening; after the sun's setting, it stands high in the 9th house. <note>In traditional astrology, the 9th house is often associated with long journeys and higher wisdom.</note>
<term>Jupiter</term> at 16 degrees, 59 minutes of <term>Pisces</term> precedes the sun, and can be seen in the mornings before it rises.
<term>Mars</term> at 11 degrees, 12 minutes of <term>Gemini</term> comes under the sun's rays and can no longer be seen.
<term>Venus</term> at 0 degrees, 57 minutes of <term>Taurus</term> enters <term>Aries</term> on the 5th of this month; after it becomes "direct" <note>original: "richtiges auffs" — referring to direct motion as opposed to retrograde.</note> on the 11th, it returns to <term>Taurus</term> on the 16th; it precedes the sun and can be seen again at the end of this month as the <term>Morning Star</term>.
<term>Mercury</term> at 20 degrees, 24 minutes of <term>Taurus</term> is <term>Retrograde</term> <note>original: "Krebs-gängig" — literally 'crab-walking,' meaning moving backward through the zodiac.</note> and in conjunction with the sun; it will become "direct" again on the 14th of this month.

->✽<-

When the <term>Sun</term> enters the 15th degree of <term>Taurus</term>, which occurs on the 6th of **May**, one should gather the herb <term>Silverweed</term> <note>original: "Benserich" — likely *Potentilla anserina*, also known as Silverweed or Wild Tansy.</note>. Press the juice from the leaves; this juice, when warmed and taken by women, drives away their "fluxes" <note>Refers to various bodily discharges or menstruation issues.</note>, stomach aches, and "tearing" <note>Cramping or sharp pains.</note> in the belly. The water distilled from the leaves serves well against heat of the lungs and liver, as well as against hot pestilential fevers, and it strengthens the weak members of the body.

Take half a <term>Loth</term> <note>A historical unit of weight, approximately 15-17 grams.</note> of dried <term>Elderberry shoots</term>; this carries away the "fluxes" that have fallen into the stomach and is a <term>Laxative</term> without any harm, though it should be used no more than once a year in May. Take seeds of <term>Marjoram</term> <note>original: "Wolgemut" — traditionally Marjoram or Oregano, believed to promote good spirits.</note> that were collected last year; you should harvest them at <term>Michaelmas</term> <note>September 29th.</note> and soak them in vinegar for several days, then press an oil from them. One spoonful taken not only promotes urine and <term>purges</term> the body, but also brings to women their "time" <note>Promotes the onset of menstruation.</note>.

<summary>This page lists the astrological positions of the planets for May 1654 and provides medical recipes for Silverweed, Elderberry shoots, and Marjoram, specifically timed to the Sun's transit through Taurus.</summary>
<keywords>astronomy, planetary houses, Taurus, Silverweed, Elderberry, Marjoram, women's health, laxatives, Michaelmas, retrograde</keywords>

<meta>This page continues the astronomical and medical guidance for the year 1654. While the previous page provided a tabular calendar for June, this page details the specific celestial positions of the planets at the month's start and offers a detailed treatise on the preparation and use of "St. John’s Oil," a staple of early modern folk medicine.</meta>

<header>Position of the planets at the start of this month. / June has 30 days.</header>

# Position of the planets at the start of this month.
## June <note>original: "Brachmonhat" — A combination of "Brachmonat" (the fallow month, an old name for June) and "hat" (has).</note> has 30 days.

The **Sun** runs in the 20th degree of **Gemini** in its decan <note>A decan is an astrological subdivision of a zodiac sign, spanning ten degrees.</note>.
**Saturn** is in the 21st degree, 32nd minute of **Leo**, moving correctly forward; it shines for two and a half hours <note>original: "dritthalbe Stund" — literally 'the third half,' an archaic way of saying 2.5.</note> after sunset.
**Jupiter**, in the 21st degree, 10th minute of **Pisces**, is somewhat held back in its course; it precedes the Sun and can be found in the 11th House before sunrise.
**Mars**, in the 2nd degree, 3rd minute of **Cancer**, its "house of fall" <note>In astrology, a 'fall' is a sign where a planet's influence is weakened.</note>, can no longer be seen due to its close proximity to the Sun.
**Venus**, in the 7th degree, 23rd minute of **Taurus**, precedes the Sun but is invisible; it enters **Gemini** on the Feast of Peter and Paul <note>June 29th.</note>.
**Mercury** enters **Gemini** on the 2nd of this month and **Cancer** on the 20th; it remains hidden beneath the Sun's rays.

---
-> * <-

The proper preparation of **St. John’s Oil**, which was also mentioned a year ago, consists of the following ingredients. Take fresh **St. John’s [Wort] flowers**, as many as you wish, put them in a glass, pour olive oil <note>original: "Baumöl" — literally "tree oil," the common term for olive oil in this period.</note> over them, cork it at the top, and place it in the sun for several days. After that, strain the oil, press the flowers well, add other fresh flowers, and place it in the sun again. Press it out once more and repeat this several times. Finally, crush the pods along with the seeds and add them to the oil; thus it becomes blood-red. This heals many wounds, especially injured tendons <note>original: "Sennadern" — archaic term for sinews or tendons.</note>. When the **Sun** enters the 10th degree of **Cancer**, which is the 3rd of July, the flowers may be preserved in sugar or made into an electuary <term>Latwerge</term> <note>A medicinal paste or syrup made by mixing powdered herbs with honey or sugar.</note>; taken a little at a time on an empty stomach, it relieves and purifies the head, drawing out all evil humors <note>original: "Flüß" — referring to "fluxes" or flowing fluids in the body.</note> and phlegm. St. John’s Wort steeped in nutmeg oil <note>original: "Muscatensl"</note> or brandy, and the oil pressed therefrom, heals old injuries. If someone is deranged or suffers from the affliction of evil spirits, one should anoint their neck, temples, and the crown of the head with this oil; anointing the temporal veins with it dispels lethargy <note>original: "Schlaffsucht" — literally "sleep-sickness."</note>. St. John’s Wort, if collected when the **Sun** and **Moon** are in **Cancer** and in the last quarter phase, and placed over pestilential boils, will cause them to break open. It also protects wounds from swelling, draws out inflammation <note>original: "Brand" — referring to the "fire" of infection or gangrene.</note>, ripens wounds well, soothes, softens, and permeates everything, and draws out the bilious water <note>original: "Gallsüchtige Wasser" — likely referring to fluid from edema or gallbladder-related ailments.</note>.

<summary>This page describes the astrological positions of the planets for June 1654 and provides a detailed recipe and medical uses for St. John's Oil, emphasizing its power to heal physical wounds and spiritual afflictions.</summary>
<keywords>astrology, planetary positions, St. John's Wort, St. John's Oil, humoral medicine, electuary, medicinal recipes, folklore</keywords>

<meta>This page follows the daily calendar for July 1654. It provides a detailed astronomical overview of the planetary positions for the beginning of the month, followed by a section on the medicinal uses of lavender, which was traditionally harvested during this time.</meta>

Position of the planets at the beginning of this month. July has 31 days.
<sig>C</sig>

<column-break/>

## **Position of the Planets at the Beginning**
### of this month.

---

## July <note>original: "Heumon" — "Hay month," a traditional German name for July.</note> has **31** days.

The Sun is, on the 1st of this month, in the 18th degree and 36th minute of Cancer, at its <term>apogee</term> <note>original: "Apogeo" — the point in the Sun's orbit where it is furthest from the Earth.</note>.

Saturn, in the 24th degree and 35th minute of Leo <note>symbol ♌</note>, is now setting <term>heliacally</term> <note>original: "heliacè" — occurring when a planet disappears into the Sun's brightness.</note> and can no longer be seen.

Jupiter, in the 22nd degree and 33rd minute of Pisces, goes <term>retrograde</term> <note>original: "zurück" — appearing to move backward from our perspective on Earth.</note> on the 3rd; when the sun rises, it is seen in the middle of the sky.

Mars, in the 21st degree and 51st minute of Cancer, meets the Sun on the 11th and passes before it; it enters Leo on the 14th and cannot be seen.

Venus, in the 2nd degree and 36th minute of Gemini, then enters Cancer on the 8th; it precedes the Sun and can be seen very well in the morning.

Mercury is in the 21st degree and 59th minute of Cancer; it enters Leo on the 6th and Virgo on the 22nd.

---

-> ❈ <-

---

Lavender is of a hot and dry nature, and this herb may also be gathered in this month; for smelling it often strengthens the eyesight. Boiled in water and a cloth soaked therein, then dried, it protects against the <term>flux</term> <note>original: "Läufen" — likely referring to diarrhea or dysentery.</note> as long as the scent remains. The flowers steeped in wine and then distilled, when held in the mouth, protect against <term>stroke</term> <note>original: "Schlag" — apoplexy or sudden paralysis.</note>. Herb and flowers baked together and burned <note>or processed</note> in the <term>Fallow Month</term> <note>original: "Brachmonat" — June.</note> is good for dizziness when the head is rubbed with it. If someone is struck by a stroke and cannot speak with their tongue, let them drink 3 <term>lots</term> <note>original: "Loht" — a historical unit of weight, roughly 15 grams or half an ounce.</note> of this water; it restores speech. It is good for <term>toothache</term> <note>original: "Zanwehe"</note> when held in the mouth, and for sores in the mouth when washed with it. There is also nothing more certain for the lameness of limbs than washing them with it. Also, drinking two lots is good for cramps, <term>cold urine</term> <note>original: "kalte Seich" — likely referring to urinary issues or incontinence</note>, for numb limbs, and for trembling of the hands.

<column-break/>

<meta>This page continues the calendar for the month of July, known as the "Hay-month." It provides astronomical data on the positions of the planets and shifts into a botanical section focusing on the medicinal properties of lavender. The layout consists of a single-column list of planetary data followed by a section of prose advice.</meta>

# July <note>original: "Hewmon" — the traditional Germanic name for the month when hay was harvested.</note> has 31 days.
C

## Position of the Planets at the Start of this Month.

The Sun is on the 1st of this month at 18 degrees, 36 minutes of Cancer in its apogee <note>original: "Apogeo" — the point in its orbit where the Sun is furthest from the Earth.</note>.
Saturn is at 24 degrees, 33 minutes of Leo; it is currently setting heliacally <note>original: "heliacè unter" — disappearing from view as it moves too close to the glare of the Sun</note> and can no longer be seen.
Jupiter is at 22 degrees, 33 minutes of Pisces; it moves retrograde <note>original: "zurück" — appearing to move backward in the sky</note> on the 3rd of this month; when the Sun rises, Jupiter is seen in the middle of the sky.
Mars is at 21 degrees, 51 minutes of Cancer; it meets the Sun on the 11th of this month and precedes it; it reaches Leo on the 14th and cannot be seen.
Venus is at 2 degrees, 56 minutes of Gemini; it enters Cancer thereafter on the 8th; it precedes the Sun and can be seen very well in the mornings.
Mercury is at 2 degrees, 59 minutes of Cancer; it enters Leo on the 6th of this month and Virgo on the 22nd.

---

-> ❈ <-

Lavender is of a hot and dry nature, and this herb may also be collected in this month; for if it is often smelled, it strengthens the sight. Boiled in water and used to dampen a <term>shirt</term> <note>Linen undergarments were often treated with herbs to repel parasites.</note> which is then dried, it keeps away lice as long as the scent remains in it. Steep the flowers in wine and distill them; if held in the mouth, this protects against a <term>stroke</term> <note>original: "Schlag" — often referring to apoplexy or sudden paralysis.</note>. The herb and flowers baked together—burn it in the <term>Fallow-month</term> <note>original: "Brachmonat" — the traditional name for June.</note>—is good for <term>dizziness</term> <note>original: "Schwindel"</note> if the head is rubbed with it. If someone is struck by a stroke and cannot speak with their tongue, let them drink 3 <term>lots</term> <note>original: "Loht" — a historical unit of weight, roughly equivalent to 14–15 grams or half an ounce.</note> of this water; it brings back speech. It is good for <term>toothache</term> <note>original: "Zanwehe"</note> when held in the mouth, or for sores in the mouth when washed with it. There is also nothing more certain for <term>lameness of the limbs</term> than to be washed with it. Also, two lots of it drunk is good for <term>cramps</term>, <term>cold urination</term> <note>original: "kalte Seich" — likely referring to strangury or painful, difficult urination.</note>, for numb limbs, and for trembling of the hands.

<keywords>astrology, planetary positions, July, lavender, herbal medicine, apoplexy, dental health, seventeenth-century science</keywords>

--- Page 29 ---
<meta>This page continues the astrological and botanical guide for the month of August. Following the calendar of feast days and daily aspects on the previous page, this section provides a detailed summary of planetary movements at the start of the month and transitions into the herbal lore for woodruff, specifically focused on liver health and cooling remedies.</meta>

August has XXXI Days.

---

# <insert>Position of the Planets at the Beginning of this Month.</insert>

The Sun enters the 18th degree, 17th minute of Leo on the 1st of this month; this is its diurnal and nocturnal house and its daily triplicity <note>In astrology, a "triplicity" is a group of three zodiac signs belonging to the same element; the Sun has special "dignity" or strength in the fire signs during the day.</note>.

Saturn: is found at the 28th degree, 24th minute of Leo; on the 14th of this month, it leaves its harmful exile <note>original: "Exilium" — an astrological state where a planet is in a sign opposite the one it rules, considered a position of weakness or "debility."</note> and enters Virgo, and on that same day, it joins with the Sun and shall henceforth precede it.

Jupiter: likewise <note>original: "Iu. it." for "Iupiter item."</note> is at the 21st degree, 16th minute of Pisces; it is still retrograde <note>appearing to move backward in the sky</note> and precedes the Sun when it rises; it stands in the 8th House and rises two hours after sunset.

Mars is at the 11th degree, 57th minute of Leo; it moves directly <note>original: "richtig fort" — moving forward through the zodiac in the standard order.</note> and enters Virgo on the 30th of this month; it cannot be seen.

Venus is at the 5th degree, 25th minute of Cancer; it enters Leo on the 23rd of this month; it can be seen very well early as the Morning Star.

Mercury is at the 15th degree, 5th minute of Virgo; it turns retrograde on the 16th of this month and joins with the Sun at the end of the month.

-> * <-

In this month, one gathers the herb called <term>Woodruff</term> <note>original: "Waldmeister," literally "Master of the Forest."</note>, commonly known as <term>Mother of the Forest</term> <note>original Latin: "Matrisylva."</note>. This herb grows with leaves arranged in a ring around a single stem, one next to the other, just like a star; it blooms white and prefers to grow in damp fields and in stony, shaded places. This herb holds drops of water upon it for a long time throughout the day; the larger its leaves are, the better it is. A drink made from this herb is excellent for a diseased <term>liver</term> and refreshes it. This herb, when placed upon hot <term>ulcers</term> <note>original: "Geschwer."</note>, cools them and drives the heat away. A plaster made from this herb, placed upon a woman’s private parts, stops discharges occurring at unnatural times. This herb, crushed with <term>barley flour</term> and <term>pomegranate wine</term>, with a little <term>rose oil</term> and white <term>sandalwood</term> added, and made into a plaster, quenches and resolves all <term>abscesses</term> <note>original: "Apostemen" — internal swellings or collections of pus.</note> of the liver. This [distilled] water, drunk in the morning, at noon, and in the evening—three or four <term>lots</term> <note>A "Loth" was a traditional unit of weight, roughly equivalent to 15-18 grams.</note> each time—is good for a hot <term>fever</term> <note>original: "Ritten," an archaic term for a fever or ague.</note>. When drunk in this manner for about 14 days, it is good for the "hot yellowing" or <term>jaundice</term> <note>original: "Geelsucht."</note>. It is also good for the liver because it strengthens and empowers it, opens its blockages, and removes unnatural heat from it when cloths are soaked in the liquid and applied to the body.

<vocab>August, Position of the Planets, Leo, Virgo, Pisces, Cancer, Woodruff, Mother of the Forest, Liver, Ulcer, Sandalwood, Abscesses, Loth (unit of weight), Fever, Jaundice</vocab>

<meta>This page continues the astronomical and botanical observations for the month of September, following the calendar grid from the previous page. # Position of the planets at the beginning of this month.
# Harvest Month <note>September</note> has 30 days.

---

The Sun is on the 1st at 18 degrees / 7 minutes of Virgo.
**Saturn** <note>original: "Saturnus"</note> in the 2nd degree, 26 minutes of Virgo is moving in its direct course and ahead of the Sun; at the end of this month, it will emerge from the Sun's rays and can be seen in the morning.
**Jupiter** in the 17th degree, 34 minutes of Pisces is in retrograde; it stands opposite the Sun and will follow it in the future.
**Mars** in the 1st degree, 45 minutes of Virgo is still beneath the Sun's rays and is therefore invisible.
**Venus** is in the 11th degree, 12 minutes of Leo as the Morning Star; it can be seen early and enters Virgo on the 17th.
**Mercury** <note>original: "Mercurius"</note> in the 13th degree, 54 minutes of Virgo ends its retrograde <note>original: "Krebsgang," literally "crab-walk"</note> on the 8th of this month and returns to a direct course, moving into Libra <note>original: "die Wag," the Scales</note> on the 28th.

->*<-

In this month, one gathers the **Serviceberries** <note>original: "Speyerling," the fruit of the Sorb Tree, *Sorbus domestica*</note>. **Pliny** <note>original: "Plinius," the Roman naturalist</note> identifies four types: the first is round like an apple, the second is shaped like a pear (both having a sweet, wine-like taste), the third is shaped like an egg, and the fourth is called the **Little Ash-Rose** <note>original: "Eschröslein"</note>. The tree grows tall with a strong trunk; the bark is smooth, of a pale yellow color, and thick. The leaves resemble laurel leaves, though they are somewhat thinner and smaller, and are serrated all around, with a long stem on both sides of the branches. The blossom is white, like that on grapes. The fruit is round in the shape of an egg; like medlars <note>original: "Mespeln"</note>, it is not eaten until it is <term>teizig</term> <note>a term for fruit that must become overripe and soft—"bletted"—before it is palatable</note>. The root is strong and thick and does not go deep into the earth. **Serviceberries**, as **Paul of Aegina** <note>original: "Paulus Achineta," a 7th-century Byzantine Greek physician</note> writes, have a constricting nature, though slightly less so than medlars. Serviceberries are baked in the autumn like wild pears in the oven. They are also preserved like quinces; some keep them green in sweet, boiled wine. When they are still yellow and unripe, they are sliced and dried in the sun to be eaten to "stop the belly" <note>to treat diarrhea</note>. A flour is also made from them, which has the same medicinal power when used in a broth. At this time, however, it is customary to bind them in clumps like long clusters of grapes and hang them in dry rooms; thus dried, they may last for a long time.

<header>Position of the planets at the beginning of this month. Wine Month <note>original: "Weinmon." A traditional German name for October, marking the grape harvest.</note> has XXXI days.</header>

# **Position of the Planets at the Start**
->of this month,<-

The Sun is in the 17th degree and 42nd minute of Libra, in its <term>fall (original: Fallhaus)</term> <note>In astrology, a "fall" is a sign where a planet's influence is considered weakened.</note>.

Saturn is in the 5th degree and 59th minute of Virgo; it precedes the Sun and can be seen very early.

Jupiter, in the 14th degree and 8th minute of Pisces, is still <term>retrograde (original: Krebsgängig)</term> <note>Literally "crab-walking," meaning the planet appears to move backward in the sky.</note>; however, on the feast day of <term>Simon and Jude (original: Simonis und Judæ)</term> <note>October 28th.</note> it will return to its correct course. When the Sun sets, it stands high in the 11th House and can be seen quite clearly, shining brightly throughout the entire night.

Mars is in the 20th degree and 42nd minute of Virgo; it also precedes the Sun and joins with Venus on the 4th of this month. Both can be seen in the morning at the beginning of the 11th House.

Venus enters Libra, her "daily house," on the 12th of this month and can, as previously mentioned, be seen early in the morning.

Mercury is in the 4th degree and 65th minute <note>The scribe likely meant 5th degree and 5 minutes, as there are only 60 minutes in a degree.</note> of Libra, its nocturnal triplicity; it enters Scorpio on the 17th and joins with the Sun on the 20th.

---

In this month, the wild cress blooms, which is otherwise commonly called <term>cuckoo flowers (original: Gauchblumen)</term>. It originates from watercress that has perhaps been displaced, or when the seed is carried by the wind or water from the brooks onto the meadows. There it grows with entirely white and flesh-colored flowers, though otherwise its leaves and flavor are like garden cress. The seed, when consumed with wine, expels a stillbirth. The seed drunk with vinegar removes swelling of the spleen. The seed eaten with honey dispels a cough and clears the chest. The seed, when chewed in the mouth and held under the tongue, removes **phlegm** <note>The original text says "Zähne" (teeth), but this is likely a scribe's error for "Zähe" (stiffness or thick phlegm), given the context of restoring speech.</note> and restores the ability to speak. The seed stops "head-flux" <note>A discharge of fluid or mucus from the head/nose.</note> or hoarseness without harm when consumed whole. The seed, when powdered and inhaled through the nose, cleanses the brain and is also good against headaches. Wild cress is good for the eyesight when the seed is boiled in wine, placed warm in a small bag, and applied to the limb. The best time to distill it is at the end of May. Wild cress water is good for <term>pustules (original: Purpeln)</term> and rashes; for those who mix it with honey, soak a white cloth in it and apply it many times—and when it becomes dry, dampen it again—it draws out the true spots.

---

## Positions of the Planets at the Beginning of this Month.

---

The Sun is at 18 degrees 37 minutes of Scorpio in its decan <note>A 'decan' is an astrological subdivision of a zodiac sign, covering ten degrees.</note>.
Saturn is at 8 degrees 44 minutes of Virgo; it precedes the Sun and stands at sunrise high in the midheaven.
Jupiter is at 13 degrees of Pisces in direct motion <note>original: "richtiges Lauffs" — meaning the planet appears to move forward from Earth's perspective, rather than in retrograde.</note>; it follows the Sun and when the Sun goes to rest, Jupiter is high in the 11th House, quite beautiful to behold.
Mars is at 10 degrees 4 minutes of Libra; it precedes the Sun and stands at sunrise high in the 11th House, and is easily recognized by its red color.
Venus is at 25 degrees of Libra and is beginning to come under the Sun’s rays; it reaches Scorpio on the 5th of this month and Sagittarius on the 29th.
Mercury is at 27 degrees 8 minutes of Scorpio, in its boundary <note>original: "Gränze" — a specific segment of a zodiac sign traditionally ruled by a particular planet.</note>; it enters Sagittarius on the 3rd of this month and Capricorn on the 24th.

->*<-

In this month blooms the <term>Hare’s-thistle</term> <note>original: "Hasenköl" or "Hasenlattich." This likely refers to the Sow-thistle (Sonchus) or a similar wild lettuce.</note>, which has a stem one cubit high, hollow and angular, and full of milky sap. The leaves are jagged like Hawkweed, the flower is yellow like Groundsel, and the seed blows away like the lettuce plants, to which family it belongs. <term>Hare’s-thistle</term> is of a cold and astringent nature, like the lettuce plants, but when it is dried, it possesses a certain heat. The sap of the <term>Hare’s-thistle</term>, when drunk, heals sores of the intestines and increases milk for nursing mothers. Applied as a suppository into the womb, it extinguishes inflammation there, as well as inflammation of the private parts. Boiled with wine and drunk, it stops diarrhea [stomach flux]. The milky sap, when expressed, is good for those who pant with a strained neck <note>Likely referring to asthma or respiratory distress.</note>; it also promotes childbirth when warmed and drunk with white wine and <unclear>oil</unclear> <note>original: "Del," likely "Oel" (oil), though printed with a character resembling a capital 'D'.</note>, and for such use the <term>Hare’s-thistle</term> should be taken. The leaves, crushed and applied, cool a hot stomach and an inflamed hip, and reduce the swelling for those with <term>gout</term> <note>original: "Podagrischen," referring to Podagra, or gout of the foot.</note>; its distilled water does the same. The sap rubbed on the eyebrows makes the hair there fall out. The herb and root applied as a poultice heal the sting of the <term>Scorpion</term>. If one places <term>Hare’s-thistle</term> under the head of someone who has a fever, without their knowledge, it is said to drive the fever away.

---

## Position of the Planets at the Beginning of This Month.
## The Winter Month <note>November</note> has 30 Days.

The Sun is at 18 degrees, 37 minutes of Scorpio in its decan <note>A decan is an astrological sub-division of a zodiac sign, covering ten degrees.</note>.
Saturn is at 8 degrees, 44 minutes of Virgo; it precedes the Sun and, at sunrise, stands high in the middle of the heavens.
Jupiter is at 13 degrees of Pisces in direct motion <note>original: "richtiges Lauffs"—meaning the planet is not in retrograde from the observer's perspective.</note>; it follows the Sun, and when the Sun goes to rest, it is high in the 11th House, a very beautiful sight to behold.
Mars is at 10 degrees, 4 minutes of Libra; it precedes the Sun and stands high in the 11th House at sunrise; it is easily recognized by its red color.
Venus is at 25 degrees of Libra; it is beginning to come under the sunbeams <note>meaning it is becoming obscured by the Sun's light</note>; it reaches Scorpio on the 5th of this month and Sagittarius on the 29th.
Mercury is at 27 degrees, 8 minutes of Scorpio, in its bounds <note>original: "Gränze"—an astrological subdivision where specific planets were thought to have more influence.</note>; it enters Sagittarius on the 3rd and Capricorn on the 24th.

---

In this month, the <term>Hare’s Cabbage</term> <note>original: "Hasenköl"</note> or <term>Hare’s Lettuce</term> <note>original: "Hasenlattich"; likely *Sonchus* or *Prenanthes purpurea*.</note> blooms. It has a stem an ell high <note>An old unit of measurement, roughly the length of an arm.</note>, hollow and angular, full of milky sap. The leaves are jagged like those of <term>Hawkweed</term> <note>original: "Habichskraut"</note>; the flower is yellow like that of <term>Groundsel</term> <note>original: "Creuzwurz"</note>; the seed blows away like that of the <term>Lettuce Herbs</term> <note>Plants in the *Lactuca* genus</note>, to which family it belongs. <term>Hare's Cabbage</term> is of a cold and astringent nature, like the lettuce herbs, but when it is dried, it possesses a warmth. The juice of the <term>Hare's Cabbage</term>, when drunk, heals injuries of the intestines and increases milk in nursing mothers. Placed into the womb <note>original: "Mutter"</note> as a suppository, it extinguishes inflammation therein, as well as inflammation of the private parts. Boiled with wine and drunk, it stops the "belly-flux" <note>diarrhea</note>. The milky sap, when pressed out, is good for those who gasp with a stretched neck <note>likely referring to asthma or labored breathing</note>; it also promotes childbirth when warmed and drunk with white wine and oil, and for such use the <term>Hare's Cabbage</term> should be collected. The leaves, crushed and applied as a poultice, cool a feverish stomach and inflamed hips, and ease the swelling of those with <term>Gout</term> <note>original: "Podagrischen"</note>; its distilled water <note>original: "gebrandt Wasser"</note> does the same. The juice rubbed onto the eyebrows causes the hair there to fall out. The herb and root, when applied, heal the sting of a <term>Scorpion</term>. If one places <term>Hare's Cabbage</term> under the head of a person who has a fever, without them knowing it, the fever is said to be driven away.

<meta>This page continues the December (Christ Month) entry of the almanac. # Position of the planets at the beginning of this month. | December has **31** days.

---

The Sun on the 1st of this month is in the 19th degree of Sagittarius, in its daily **triplicity** <note>original: "Triplicitåt"; a group of three zodiac signs belonging to the same element, in this case, Fire</note>.

Saturn, at 10 degrees 2 minutes of Virgo, becomes **retrograde** <note>original: "Krebsgängig," literally "walking like a crab"; the apparent backward motion of a planet</note> on the 10th of this month; it precedes the Sun, and when the Sun rises, it stands high in the 9th House.

Jupiter, at 14 degrees 00 minutes of Pisces, is back in **direct motion** <note>original: "richtiges lauffs"</note>; it follows the Sun, and when the Sun sets, it is high in the 10th House, quite beautiful to behold.

Mars, at 28 degrees 38 minutes of Libra, enters its sign of Scorpio on the 3rd, in which it has great strength; it precedes the Sun, and when the Sun rises, it stands high in the 10th House and can be seen very clearly.

Venus, at 5 degrees 23 minutes of Sagittarius, enters Capricorn on the 22nd of this month; it cannot be seen.

Mercury, at 7 degrees 40 minutes of Capricorn, in its **term** <note>original: "Gräntze"; a specific subdivision of a zodiac sign where a planet was thought to have influence</note>, turns retrograde on the 10th of this month and returns to direct motion exactly on New Year's Day.

---

->*<-

---

In this month, in warm countries, one gathers **Hound’s-tongue** <note>original: "Hundszungen"; the herb Cynoglossum officinale</note>; it is, however, cold and dry in the second degree <note>referring to the Galenic system of humors where plants were classified by their "temperature"</note>. Hound’s-tongue, crushed and roasted with hog's lard, is good when applied to burns and rabid dog bites. Boiled with wine and drunk, it induces bowel movements. The juice, gently boiled with **honey of roses** <note>original: "Rosenhonig"; a common medicinal preparation of honey infused with rose petals</note> until the juice is somewhat consumed, then stirred and allowed to cool, serves for ulcers arising from cold **fluxes** <note>original: "Flüssen"; a discharge of bodily humors</note>; applied externally, it heals "French sores" <note>original: "Französische Schäden"; a common historical term for syphilis</note> and swellings. At the end of **June** <note>original: "Brachmonats"; the month for breaking fallow ground</note>, distil this herb with all its substance; it is good for **hemorrhoids** <note>original: "Feigwartzen"; literally "fig-warts," referring to external growths or piles</note>. Where they are internal, one should drink three **Loth** <note>original: "Loht"; a historical German unit of weight, roughly 15 grams per Loth</note> of the water every morning until they disappear; where they are external, wash them with it, soak a linen cloth in it and apply it, and they will vanish. Used thus morning and evening, it is good for all wounds and old injuries.

---

# Concerning the Eclipses of this Year 1654.
## Year.

IN this year, there will again occur 4 eclipses of the two great lights of the heavens <note>The "great lights" refers to the Sun and the Moon</note>, namely 2 of the Sun and 2 of the Moon. Among these, we shall be able to see the latter one of the Sun and the latter one of the Moon, while the others must be left to other peoples to witness.

As far as the 2 lunar eclipses are concerned, the first of which will occur on March 3rd New Style (February 21st Old Calendar) and the other on August 17th (27th), there is only this much to note: they will cause little harm because the first will be eclipsed by only 3 <term>digits</term> <note>original: "Finger breit"; a traditional astronomical measurement where the diameter of the celestial body is divided into 12 "fingers" or digits</note> and the other by only 2 digits. Therefore, I do not wish to make myself go gray with worry in describing them. It is also unnecessary for them to be large and terrifying or to help increase the prevailing evils, since the cruel darkening of the Sun, which we shall perceive twice this year, will cause us enough trouble. We shall now proceed to describe these.

Thus, initially and for the first time, the dear Sun shall be entirely robbed of its light and luster on February 7/17, specifically at 6 o'clock in the morning. At that time, the Sun and Moon will be joined in the 28th degree of <term>Aquarius</term> near the <term>Dragon's Head</term> <note>original: "Drachenhaubt"; the North Node of the Moon, the point where the Moon's orbit crosses the ecliptic</note>. As mentioned, we will not be able to see this one, but rather those who dwell far toward the South <note>original: "Mittag"; literally "midday," but traditionally used to mean the cardinal direction of South</note> will see it, because at that time the Sun will not yet have risen for us. Now, although this eclipse causes a change in all things because it occurs in the last <term>decan</term> <note>original: "Decano"; a 10-degree subdivision of a zodiac sign</note> of Aquarius, it nevertheless signifies that those who have endured much evil in the...

<summary>The author introduces the four eclipses predicted for 1654, dismissing the minor lunar eclipses to focus on two significant solar eclipses, the first of which occurs in Aquarius.</summary>
<keywords>astronomy, 1654, solar eclipse, lunar eclipse, Aquarius, Dragon's Head, astrology, digits of eclipse</keywords>

--- Page 41 ---
<meta>This page continues the discussion of the astronomical forecasts for 1654, specifically focusing on the mitigation of the first solar eclipse and the dire predictions for the major solar eclipse of August 1654. The previous page ended mid-sentence regarding the first solar eclipse of February.</meta>

in similar cases not go completely to ruin / but rather should be saved / because specifically **Jupiter** also unites with the two celestial lights <note>The Sun and the Moon</note> at the point of this eclipse / and such also occurs in **Pisces** as his dwelling <note>In traditional astrology, Jupiter is the planetary ruler of the zodiac sign Pisces</note> / and thus he wishes to mitigate and soothe the evil. Would to God now / that it were of a similar nature to that great **solar eclipse** / which we shall get to see / and indeed on the 2nd (12th) of August <note>The date is given in both the Old Style (Julian) and New Style (Gregorian) calendars</note> / regarding which the conditions will be thus: the **Sun** and **Moon** will unite on that same day in the 19th degree and 33rd minute of **Leo** near the poisonous **Dragon’s Tail** <note>original: "Drachenschwanz"; the South Node of the Moon, traditionally viewed as a point of negative or depleting influence</note>. The beginning occurs at half past 9 o'clock in the morning / the middle at 11 o'clock / and the end at half past twelve. Now, during the greatest darkening, the two celestial lights will stand in the middle of the sky at the top in the 10th House <note>The "10th House" or Midheaven represents the highest point in the sky, often associated with kings and public authority</note>; before them stands the mad **Mars** in the 12th degree of **Cancer** / and behind them **Saturn** in the 28th degree of the aforementioned sign / because such an eclipse has not happened in many hundreds of years / it is to be gathered from this / into what misery / fear and need those shall be plunged / who live under the two heavenly signs of **Leo** and **Aquarius** / since it is notorious and well-known / that / as often as a solar eclipse occurs in the second **Decan** <note>original: "Decano"; a ten-degree subdivision of a zodiac sign</note> of **Leo** / such events are always accustomed to plunge high heads <note>A reference to kings, princes, and leaders</note> into great misery, tribulation, and heartache. Therefore, it will be said here according to the warning of **Christ**: **Watch and pray / so that you do not fall into temptation** <note>A reference to Matthew 26:41</note> / just as such things shall be carried out more extensively elsewhere.

---

->**GOD** / who dwells in such a light / where no one can come / wishes to give us instead of this temporal light the eternal light / for the sake of him / who in **Malachi** at the 4th chapter is called the **Sun of Righteousness** / who is **Jesus Christ / yesterday and today / the same also for eternity / Amen.**<-

<meta>This page transitions from specific astronomical forecasts for the year 1654 into a practical guide for medical health, likely as part of an annual almanac. ### Brief Instruction /

# On Purgation and Other Medicine / also
## Bloodletting, Bathing, and Cupping.

->On Medicine.<-

**A**lthough a physician <note>original: "Medicus"</note> must primarily consider the patient's condition and necessity—to the point that he often must not look for or wait for any celestial sign, because necessity has no law, as they say—experience nevertheless shows that celestial signs are also useful to observe.
1. When the Moon is in the **fixed signs** <note>original: "signis fixis"</note>, such as **Taurus, Leo, Scorpio,** or **Aquarius**, purging medicine has little effect, even if it is prepared quite strongly.
2. When the Moon is in the **ruminant signs** <note>original: "signis ruminantibus"; signs representing animals that chew cud</note>, such as **Aries, Taurus,** or **Capricorn**, one should not take any purging medicine, for one must generally vomit it up again soon.
3. If one wishes to take **emetics** <note>original: "vomitiva"; medicine to induce vomiting</note>, or medicine that causes one to break, one should do it in the aforementioned signs, and it will proceed successfully.
4. In winter, it is better to purge through the bowels; in summer, through vomiting. However, one must see what the illness requires.
5. In great cold and heat, medicines harm our bodies; therefore, caution is also to be used at those times.
6. In the cleansing of the blood and the liver, **Jupiter** should be weak. In the cleansing of the heart, the **Sun** should not be too powerful. In the expulsion of bile and the cleansing of the kidneys, **Mars** should also be found to be powerless. If one wishes to purge and cleanse the chest and lungs, **Mercury** should also not be too strong. But if you wish to purge the head, the **Moon** must be found to be weak. If physicians observe this, they can successfully remedy many a heavy ailment, as **Ptolemy** also says: *A person skilled in the affairs of heaven can avert many effects if they prepare themselves or others before their arrival.* <note>original: "Rerum cœli peritus multos effectus potest avertere, si se aliosve ante illorum adventum præparaverit."</note> He who diligently takes heed of the course of the heavens can flee many harmful things, etc.
7. The best time for medicine is also when the Moon moves in the **watery signs**, especially in **Cancer** and **Pisces**. In **Scorpio**, it is not as good.
8. When **Jupiter** is conjoined with the **Moon**, purgation works very little, for the nature of man is particularly strong at that time.
9. In **Cancer**, it is good to purge through **electuaries** <note>original: "Lattwergen"; a medicinal paste made with honey or syrup</note>; in **Pisces**, through pills; in **Scorpio**, through drinks; in **Scorpio** and **Libra**, through **clysters** <note>original: "Clistier"; an early form of enema</note>.
10. In **Taurus** (♉), **Capricorn** (♑), and **Virgo** (♍), it is best to take **strengthening medicines** <note>original: "confortantia"; remedies intended to fortify the organs</note>.
11. In **Gemini, Libra,** and **Aquarius**, it is good to use **digestives** <note>original: "digestiva"</note>, which aid digestion and "cook" or prepare the bodily moisture <note>Refers to the maturation of the body's humors according to the medical theories of the time</note>.

<summary>This page provides a guide for timing medical treatments like purgatives and emetics according to the position of the Moon and planets in the zodiac. It blends traditional humoral medicine with astrology, citing Ptolemy to justify the influence of the heavens on human health.</summary>
<keywords>purgation, medicine, bloodletting, cupping, astrology, zodiac signs, Ptolemy, Jupiter, Mars, emetics, humors</keywords>

<meta>This page continues the practical medical advice from the previous section on purgation, moving into specific guidelines for bloodletting, cupping, and bathing. It maintains the same structure of numbered rules based on astrological timing and the four humors—Sanguine, Choleric, Phlegmatic, and Melancholic—which were the foundation of medicine in the 17th century.</meta>

### On Bloodletting.

1. **A**lthough the almanac lists certain days that are good for bloodletting and cupping <note>term: "cupping" (Schrepffen) – a practice where heated glass cups were applied to the skin to create suction and draw out "bad" humors</note>, one should still note that no limb should be struck with the lancet <note>original: "Flitze"</note> when the Moon is occupying or passing through the zodiac sign governing that part of the body. Since Aries <note>original: "Wider"</note> governs the head, you should neither bleed nor cup the head while the Moon is in Aries. Taurus governs the neck. Gemini governs the shoulders, arms, and hands. Cancer governs the lungs, stomach, and spleen. Leo governs the heart and back. Virgo governs the belly and the intestines or guts. Libra governs the kidneys and bladder. Scorpio governs the private parts. Sagittarius governs the hips. Capricorn governs the kneecaps. Aquarius governs the shins. Pisces governs the feet. The specific sign the Moon passes through each day is recorded in order within the calendar.

2. In the spring and summer, one should let blood on the right side. In the autumn and winter, on the left side. *Spring and summer the right, autumn and winter the left* <note>original Latin: "Ver, æstas dextras, autumnus, hyemsq́; ſiniſtras"</note> is the old verse.

3. After the New Moon, young people who are over 14 years old and of a Sanguine temperament <note>term: "Sanguine" – characterized by an abundance of blood, believed to make a person cheerful and active</note> should undergo bloodletting. After the First Quarter, those of mature age and Choleric temperament. After the Full Moon, old people and Phlegmatic temperaments. After the Last Quarter, the very elderly and Melancholic temperaments.

4. In a fiery sign <note>Aries, Leo, Sagittarius</note> it is good to bleed those of Phlegmatic temperament—specifically in Aries or Sagittarius, but not in Leo. In airy signs <note>Libra, Aquarius, Gemini</note> it is good for the Melancholics—such as in Libra or Aquarius, but not in Gemini, nor in the last 17 degrees of Libra. In watery signs <note>Cancer, Scorpio, Pisces</note> it is good for the Cholerics—especially in Cancer and Pisces. In the earthy signs <note>Taurus, Virgo, Capricorn</note> it is not as beneficial.

5. When a person is over forty years old, it is not advisable to bleed the cephalic vein <note>original: "Hauptader"; the large vein in the arm often used for bloodletting</note> unless there is a great necessity. After fifty years, do not bleed the median vein <note>original: "Median"; the middle vein of the arm</note> either. He who is sixty years old should no longer let blood, but rather keep the blood as a treasure of life <note>original: "Schatz deß Lebens"</note>. However, cases of extreme necessity are excepted.

6. After bloodletting, one should—especially on the first day—be moderate in food and drink, and not immediately "guzzle" the veins full again. On the second day, one may eat a bit more. On the third and fourth days, one should still remain still and avoid heavy labor. On the fifth day, one may exercise again. On the sixth day, one may bathe.

---

### On Bathing and Cupping.

**W**hen the Moon is in Capricorn, Taurus, or Virgo, it is not an ideal time to bathe, because at those times the pores or sweat-holes <note>original: "Pori oder Schweißlöcher"</note> are closed. 2. In Aries, Leo, and Sagittarius, it is good to bathe, for then the pores are open; however, it is not good to cup in Leo. 3. In Cancer, Pisces, and Scorpio, it is good to bathe. In Aquarius, Gemini, and Libra as well; however, in Gemini, it is not good to cup. Such rules are useful and well worth remembering, which is why I have gladly chosen to set them down here. May Almighty GOD grant us a healthy, peaceful, and joyful year, and graciously turn and change all evil, for the sake of JESUS Christ,

-> Amen, Amen, Amen. <-

<meta>The previous page served as the title page and introduction to Marx Friederich Rosencreutzer’s 1654 work. It established his identity as a "Lover of the Natural Magical Arts" and set the stage for a calendar of plants and their astrological influences. This page begins the actual instructional content, starting with the first and highest planet, Saturn. The catchword "Satur-" from the previous page's signature marks the transition to this chapter.</meta>

# New Practice.

## The First Chapter: Of Saturn. ♄.

Saturn, as the uppermost planet, is an exceedingly powerful and potent star. He was placed by the Almighty Creator as the highest in the heavens among all planets, at the greatest distance from us, and is twenty-two times larger than the entire globe or sphere of the Earth encompasses. He is of a very slow gait, for in thirty years he traverses the twelve signs of the zodiac only a single time. He does not shine with a constant magnitude or brightness of light, but rather changes according to how he is positioned against the Sun or situated in relation to it.

He has been painted or depicted by the ancient philosophers or naturalists in the form of an exceedingly withered, old, hoarse, and unfriendly man, with a long ice-gray beard and snow-white hair, and small, dark, poisonous eyes. In one hand he holds a sharp sickle, and in the other a child, as if he intended to constantly shove it into his maw and devour it <note>This refers to the mythological figure of Saturn (Cronus) who, according to legend, ate his children to prevent them from usurping his power.</note>. He also has only one whole leg, while the other is shortened, so that he must limp upon a crutch. He is not always visible in the heavens. Libra (♎) is his exaltation <note>term: "Exaltation" — In astrology, this is the specific zodiac sign where a planet is thought to have its most beneficial or strongest influence.</note>, wherein he possesses great power; Capricorn (♑) and Aquarius (♒) are each his own house, and these are depicted on his two wheels.

By this, his exceedingly terrible and adverse malice is indicated—especially his deadly quality toward humans and other useful creatures of God. For through his small, poisonous eyes, not only are his weak radiance and warmth indicated, but also that his base, subordinate plants are commonly harmful to the eyes and cause blindness.

### Signatures, Attributes, and Characteristics of Saturnine Plants. <note>term: "Signatures" — This refers to the "Doctrine of Signatures," a historical belief that God marked every plant with a physical sign (shape, color, or location) to indicate its medicinal use or its planetary ruler.</note>

Firstly, regarding their location: they are found in wild, uncultivated, stinking, ugly, and quite abominable places—in summary, locations entirely contrary to mankind. Whether they be the highest mountains or deepest valleys, cliffs, wildernesses, thickets, or swamps, they are generally exceedingly cold, where commonly no Sun (☉) or few people can reach, and where otherwise nothing friendly, joyful, or useful grows. 

2. Their form is withered, meager, and very unlovely to behold, so that Nature herself commonly warns mankind against them and gives him a sense of disgust or horror (as a sign to guard himself against them). Nevertheless, this is not true of all cases, for even the Devil sometimes disguises himself as an Angel of Light, and not all that glitters is gold. 

3. The flower is rarely beautiful—excepting the <term>Wolfsbane <note>original Latin: "Napellum," likely referring to Aconitum napellus</note></term>—or charmingly colored, but rather deadly, blackish, stinking, and abominable. 

4. The scent is unlovely. 

5. The taste is astringent <note>The text uses "zusammenziehend," meaning it causes a puckering sensation in the mouth.</note>, grayish, and repugnant.
